#### Part 1 notes (2026-10-24) - what changed in `PdfViewerActivity.kt`
- **Failed pages are no longer permanent.** `failedPages` is now `page -> time of failure` plus `failCount`. A failed page is retried after 3 s (when it is on screen), up to 3 times; after that the blank page shows "Page N can't be shown (reason) / Tap to retry" and a tap on the viewer retries every visible failed page (`retryFailedVisible`). `showPages` (rotation / width change) and `begin` clear the failure state (before, rotation kept it).
- **Real reason is kept.** Render exceptions are logged (`Log.w`, tag `PdfViewer`: page, size, exception) and shown in a Toast in debuggable builds. Sizes are tried at full, 1/2, 1/4 before a page counts as failed (OOM also clears the bitmap cache).
- **Race fix (most likely cause of "page 1 only").** `onViewRecycled` could remove a page from `bound` AFTER a new holder had bound the same page, so the render task skipped it and nothing asked again (white until the next scroll stop). Now a recycled holder only removes the mark when no other holder owns that page, and a skipped task re-requests (and repairs `bound`) when the page is visible.
- **Bad page boxes.** NaN / zero sizes in `openDoc` fall back to A4 (and are logged); `renderSize` treats a bad ratio as A4.
- **Overview grid.** `requestThumb` logs failures and retries once at half size (thumbnails no longer stay blank after one exception).
- Unchanged on purpose: `renderPage` locking (`rlock`) was already correct.
- **Device test still open (from the Part 1 brief):** a 20+ page PDF from Word and one from LibreOffice, fling to the end and back with the scroller, rotate, overview grid shows all thumbnails, a 100-page PDF.

#### Part 2 notes (2026-10-24) - Exact pages through the PC
- **New `core/OfficeExact.kt`.** `eligible(name)` (doc docx rtf odt xls xlsx ods ppt pptx odp; not csv), `mode(ctx)` / `setMode`, `cached`, `convert(ctx, src, quick, status)`, `md5`. Cache `cache/office-pdf/<md5 of the file CONTENT>.pdf`, 12 h, 300 MB cap, oldest first. Keyed by content (not path / time stamp) because the viewer re-downloads remote files on every open, and an edited file converts again by itself. One conversion at a time on the phone (`lock`); pcprint.py also serialises (`CONVERT_LOCK`), so a busy PC just makes the phone wait (phone read timeout 180 s).
- **Setting** is NOT in `Cfg` (deviation from the Part 2 brief, simpler): SharedPreferences `office_view`, key `mode` = `auto` (default) | `exact` | `simple`; `OfficeExact.setMode(ctx, ...)`. `auto` and `exact` behave the same today; Part 13 adds the Settings row.
- **`OfficeViewerActivity`:** `onCreate` -> unless the intent has `simple=true` or mode is `simple`, and the file is eligible, `exactOrSimple(f, auto=true)`: shows "Preparing exact pages..." (Back = cancel), uses the cache or converts on a background thread, then sets `PdfViewerActivity.pending` ({name, file, size, key `office|<md5>`, office:{path, dev, rpath}}), starts the PDF viewer and finishes. On ANY failure: one toast with the real reason ("Simple view - ..." when automatic) and the old HTML view loads. After a "no PC" failure, automatic opens skip the PC search for 60 s (`failWhy` / `failAt`) so each file does not wait 3.5 s again. Menu item **Show exact pages (PC)** (id 10) forces a try.
- **`PdfViewerActivity`:** `begin()` accepts a `file` (absolute path that must be inside `cacheDir`) instead of a localhost `url`; it skips the download and uses the file directly (not cached / not deleted). When the JSON has `office`, a small **Aa** button (top bar, `simpleBtn`) reopens the original in the HTML view with `simple=true` (keeps `dev` / `rpath`, so editing works there) and closes the PDF.
- **Editing:** unchanged, HTML view only. After an edit the content md5 differs, so the next open converts the edited file.
- **Not done / by design:** no progress percentage during the upload / conversion; opening from outside the app (VIEW / SEND intents) also tries exact pages in auto mode; `.pptx` / `.odp` go through the same route (bonus, untested).
- **Device test (open):** (1) PC with pcprint.py v11+ and Word or LibreOffice on the same Wi-Fi: open a .docx and a .xlsx from the file list -> real pages in the PDF viewer, **Aa** opens the simple view with Find and Edit working; open the same file again -> instant (cache). (2) PC off: the simple view opens after about 4 s with a toast that says why; a second file opens without the 3.5 s wait. (3) Menu **Show exact pages (PC)** from the simple view. (4) Edit a docx in the simple view, reopen -> the PDF shows the edit. (5) Old pcprint.py -> toast "update pcprint.py".

#### Part 3 notes (2026-10-24) - Fonts
- **Fonts bundled (deviation from the brief: size and source).** 20 TTFs in `assets/fonts/office/`: Carlito, Caladea (4 styles each), Liberation Sans / Serif / Mono (4 each), plus `LICENSES/` (Carlito-OFL, Caladea-OFL, Liberation-OFL; all SIL OFL 1.1). The sandbox could NOT reach GitHub or npm (proxy 403), so the files come from the sandbox's installed Debian packages (fonts-crosextra-carlito 20230309-2 (Carlito 1.104), fonts-crosextra-caladea 20200211-2 (Caladea 1.001), fonts-liberation 1:2.1.5-3 (Liberation 2.1.5)), copied **unmodified**. Size is **7.2 MB raw, not about 3 MB**: they are the full upstream fonts; the APK deflates TTFs, so expect roughly +3.5-4 MB. They were NOT subset on purpose: OFL Reserved Font Names (Carlito, Liberation) forbid a modified font under the same name. If the size matters, the safe cuts are the 4 Mono files (1.2 MB) and the BoldItalic files (fonts are loaded lazily, so unused ones cost only APK size).
- **Fonts were never read before.** `DocxReader` / `XlsxReader` ignored font names; only the CSS stack `Calibri,Carlito,Arial` existed (and no Carlito on the phone, so every document showed in the phone's default font). Now: `DRun.font`, `DDoc.defaultFont` (rPrDefault), run + style + theme resolution (`w:rFonts` ascii / hAnsi first, else asciiTheme / hAnsiTheme -> `majorFont` / `minorFont` Latin typeface from `word/theme/theme1.xml`). `DRun.sameStyle` now compares the font (adjacent runs with different fonts are no longer merged). Excel: `XFont.name` (new last constructor parameter with default null, so other callers compile); xlsx `<name val>` and BIFF8 FONT record (`XlsReader`). NOT done: `.doc` (binary Word), `.odt` / `.ods`, PowerPoint run fonts, East-Asian / complex-script fonts (`eastAsia`, `cs`).
- **`core/office/FontMap.kt`** (no `android.*`): `stack(name)` -> e.g. `Carlito,Calibri,Arial,sans-serif`; unknown name -> `'Name',serif|monospace|sans-serif` (guess by name); `clean()` strips quotes / comma lists / unsafe characters (the value goes inside `style="..."`); `fontFaceCss(urlBase)` = 20 `@font-face` rules (a face downloads only when text uses it); `assetPath(file)` = whitelist for the server side. Aliases: Calibri, Calibri Light -> Carlito; Cambria -> Caladea; Arial, Helvetica -> Liberation Sans; Times New Roman, Times -> Liberation Serif; Courier New, Courier -> Liberation Mono. New Office default fonts (Aptos etc.) have no twin and use the generic fallback.
- **Serving to the WebView (the CORS risk): solved by not using a custom scheme.** `lsoffice://` fonts would hit Chromium's rule that CORS-mode requests (fonts) only work for http / https / data. Instead the page is loaded with `loadDataWithBaseURL("https://appassets.androidplatform.net/", ...)` (`FontMap.BASE`) and `OfficeViewerActivity`'s `WebViewClient.shouldInterceptRequest` answers `https://appassets.androidplatform.net/office-fonts/<file>.ttf` from assets (same origin as the page -> no CORS check; the header `Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *` is sent anyway). Other requests are untouched. In-page `#anchor` links to the same base are allowed in `shouldOverrideUrlLoading`. The "Preparing..." status page still uses `about:blank`. **If a device shows the fallback font:** (a) `adb logcat | grep -i chromium` for a blocked font request; (b) the planned fallback is base64 `data:` URIs in the CSS for the families the document uses (not built).
- **`DocxHtml.render(..., fontBase)` / `XlsxHtml.render(..., fontBase)`**: new optional last parameter, default null = no `@font-face` (used by `core/OfficeWeb.kt`, the browser view, unchanged: it still gets the stack, so a PC with Carlito / Liberation shows them). Body font = default font of the document through FontMap; a run / cell gets its own `font-family` only when it differs from the default. **Reading mode keeps its own reading font** (`Noto Serif`): run fonts are ignored there; Part 5 (print layout) is where document fonts really show in Word files.
- **Self-test:** `java -cp <classes>:kotlin-stdlib MainKt --fonts tools/office_selftest app/src/main/assets/fonts/office` (new fixtures `sample_fonts.docx` with theme + style + explicit fonts, `sample_fonts.xlsx` made by openpyxl). Compile tip for this sandbox (no `kotlinc` binary): `java -cp <gradle lib>/kotlin-compiler-embeddable-2.0.21.jar:kotlin-stdlib:kotlin-script-runtime:kotlin-reflect:kotlin-daemon-embeddable:trove4j:annotations:kotlinx-coroutines-core-jvm org.jetbrains.kotlin.cli.jvm.K2JVMCompiler -no-stdlib -cp kotlin-stdlib.jar <sources> -d <outdir>` (`-include-runtime` fails there; run with stdlib on the classpath).
- **Device test (open):** (1) install, open a .docx written in Calibri in **Simple view** (the **Aa** button or menu): text is Carlito, not the phone font; a Cambria heading is Caladea; Arial / Times / Courier parts are Liberation Sans / Serif / Mono. (2) Compare with the "Exact pages" PDF of the same file: same words at the start and end of the first 10 lines (in Print layout; reading mode reflows by design). (3) An .xlsx with Calibri and an Arial column: fonts differ per column. (4) APK size grew by about 3.5-4 MB. (5) Tapping a footnote number in a docx still jumps (anchors on the new base URL) and sheet tabs still switch.

#### Part 4 notes (2026-10-24) - Word page model (no UI)
- **New `DSection`** (in `DocxModel.kt`, all lengths in POINTS = twips / 20): `pgW pgH orient marT marB marL marR gutter hdrDist ftrDist cols colGap colSep colWidths titlePg evenOdd breakType pgNumStart pgNumFmt`, six header / footer lists (`headerDefault / headerFirst / headerEven`, `footerDefault / footerFirst / footerEven`), `startsAtBlockIndex` (index into `DDoc.body`). Extras beyond the brief, cheap and needed by Part 5: `gutter`, `colSep`, `colWidths` (only when `equalWidth=0`), `breakType` (w:type), `pgNumStart` / `pgNumFmt` (w:pgNumType), `contentW` / `contentH`, `headerFor(firstOfSection, pageNo)` / `footerFor(...)` (titlePg -> first, evenOdd + even page -> even, else default). Missing values use Word's defaults (Letter, 1 inch margins, header / footer 0.5 inch, gap 0.5 inch). `orient` = the `w:orient` attribute, else derived from width > height.
- **`DDoc.sections`** (always >= 1 for .docx; EMPTY for .doc / .odt / .pptx / ...: use `DDoc.sectionAt(blockIndex)`, which returns a default Letter section then) and `DDoc.evenOdd` (`w:evenAndOddHeaders` from `word/settings.xml`, copied into every section).
- **Parsing (`DocxReader`).** A paragraph-level `w:sectPr` (inside `w:pPr`) ends a section; the body-level one is the last. A section starts at the body block index after the previous one ended (text-box paragraphs emitted after the host paragraph stay in the host's section). `sectPr` inside tables, headers, notes or text boxes is ignored. Header / footer parts are read once per relationship id; **a section without its own reference inherits the previous section's** (per type, as Word does); section 1 without one = empty.
- **Compatibility.** `DDoc.header` / `DDoc.footer` still exist: now "default header / footer of the first section that has one" (before: first default reference found anywhere; same result for normal files). The HTML view is unchanged apart from fields (below).
- **Fields.** `DRun.FIELD` (= 6) with `DRun.field` = `PAGE` | `NUMPAGES` | `DATE` | `TITLE` and `text` = the cached result, style copied from the first result run. Both `w:fldSimple` and `w:fldChar` / `w:instrText` (begin / separate / end, nesting handled) are recognised; the result runs are replaced by ONE FIELD run. Any other field type (SEQ, HYPERLINK, TOC, PAGEREF ...) is untouched: its cached result stays ordinary TEXT runs as before. `DPara.text` / `plainText` include the FIELD text; `DocxHtml` prints the cached result like text (Part 5 must replace PAGE / NUMPAGES per page itself). A field state is per paragraph (text boxes inside a field do not disturb it); a field that never ends keeps its plain runs.
- **Fixture / test.** `tools/office_selftest/sample_sections.docx`: 3 sections (A4 portrait; Letter landscape, 2 columns with separator, different first page, own footer with PAGE + DATE; Letter portrait with no references = full inheritance), `evenAndOddHeaders` on, header with a TITLE field, footer with a PAGE `fldSimple` and a complex NUMPAGES, plus an unrecognised complex SEQ field. Run: `kotlinc app/src/main/java/com/lanshare/app/core/office/*.kt tools/office_selftest/Main.kt -include-runtime -d /tmp/ost.jar` then `java -jar /tmp/ost.jar --sections tools/office_selftest`. Kotlin compiler for the sandbox: `https://github.com/JetBrains/kotlin/releases/download/v2.0.21/kotlin-compiler-2.0.21.zip` (works now; earlier sessions reported a 403).
- **Known limits.** Not parsed: `w:vAlign`, line numbering, page borders, `w:docGrid`, mirrored margins (`w:mirrorMargins`), `gutterAtTop`, header / footer of `.doc` / `.odt`. Units other than twips (strict-OOXML "1in") are ignored (default kept). No Android code and no UI changed, so there is nothing new to test on a device in this part.

#### Part 5 notes (2026-10-24) - Print layout
- **Spike 5a (done, verdict above).** Multi-column trick works: each CSS column of a fixed-height container = one page, the browser breaks lines / paragraphs / table rows itself. Pages are stacked VERTICALLY (no horizontal paging needed): each white sheet shows its own column of a CLONE of the flow (translated by -page x stride inside an overflow:hidden box); clones exist only for sheets near the screen (IntersectionObserver, rootMargin 60 percent), others are empty shells. Page count = `ceil((scrollWidth - 1) / stride)`. Pinch zoom stays sharp (viewport meta `width` = widest page; WebView re-rasterises).
- **Files.** `DocxHtml.renderPrint(doc, image, title, fontBase)` (new, plus a thread-local print flag read by the shared `para()` / `table()`), new `DocxPrintJs.kt` (the one script; no `$` in it), `DocxModel.kt` (`DPara.spBefore / spAfter / lineMul / lineExact / lineAtLeast`), `DocxReader.kt` (parses `w:spacing`, now also `docDefaults/pPrDefault`), `FontMap.lineFactor()`, `OfficeViewerActivity.kt`. Self-test `--print X.docx out.html`, `--printtest`; browser check `tools/office_selftest/print_layout_chromium.py`; fixture `tools/office_selftest/sample_pages.docx`.
- **Viewer.** Menu item "Print layout (pages)" (pref `office_read` / `print`, **default OFF** until device-tested; reading mode and print layout exclude each other). All loads go through `show()`: JavaScript is enabled ONLY for the print page of a Word-type document; any http(s) request to a host other than the font host is answered 403 (no network from the page); in-page `#links` (footnotes) are mapped to the right page by the script. If the script throws, the same HTML shows as continuous white sheets ("paper mode").
- **Layout rules.** Flows = consecutive sections with identical geometry and `continuous` break share one flow; every other section starts a new page with its own size / margins / columns. Headers / footers: first-page, even / odd, `PAGE` (with `pgNumStart` / `pgNumFmt`) and `NUMPAGES` fields replaced per page. Paragraph spacing: before / after in points (max of adjoining, as Word), line spacing = multiple x the font's natural height (`FontMap.lineFactor`), headings keep-with-next, `pageBreakBefore` and page-break runs = column break, table rows do not split. Formats without sections (.doc, .odt) use A4 with 2.5 cm margins.
- **Honest limits.** Header taller than the top margin overlaps the text (Word pushes the text down); evenPage / oddPage section breaks add no blank page; unequal column widths become equal; footnotes collected after the last paragraph; body fields PAGE not updated; floating images / text boxes (Part 6) and tab stops / table styles (Part 7) not yet; line-perfect equality with Word is not promised (Exact pages stays the reference). Long documents (100+ pages): each live sheet holds a clone of its whole flow, so memory / layout time grows with length; if slow on the phone, the fix is to split the flow into per-page chunks (not done).
- **Device test still open.** Print layout ON, a 10+ page Word file: sheets fit the screen width, fling scroll is smooth, pinch zoom sharp, page numbers / header / footer right, a landscape section, footnote link jumps to its page, rotate screen, memory with a 60+ page file. Then compare the page count with Exact pages.

#### Part 6 notes (2026-10-24) - Floating images, text boxes, shapes
- **Model (`DocxModel.kt`).** `DAnchor` (wrap `square | tight | tb | none`, `wrapText`, `behind`, `relH` page / margin / column / character, `relV` page / margin / paragraph / line, `hOff / vOff` EMU, `hAlign / vAlign`, `distT/B/L/R`, `z`); `DImage.anchor` (null = inline); new `DShape` (anchor, size, `geom` rect / roundRect / ellipse / line, fill, outline colour + width + dash, rotation, flips, text-box `blocks`, insets, group offset `dx / dy`); new run kind `DRun.SHAPE` (`DRun.shape`). `DDoc.plainText` also walks the shape blocks, so text-box text stays searchable.
- **Reader (`DocxReader.parseDrawing`, rewritten; `parseWsp`, `parseVml`, helpers).** Reads `wp:anchor` / `wp:inline`, `positionH / V` (`posOffset`, `align`, `relativeFrom`), the four wrap elements, `behindDoc`, dist attributes, `relativeHeight`; `wps:wsp` (preset geometry, solid fill / no fill, outline, dash, rotation, flips, `style` fillRef / lnRef theme colour as fallback, `bodyPr` insets, `txbx` content parsed with the normal block parser); group shapes (`wpg:wgp`) are flattened, each child scaled from the group's child space (pictures inside a group get the group's anchor, no own offset). VML (`w:pict`): `v:shape / rect / roundrect / oval / line` with `style` (position:absolute, margin-left / top, width / height, z-index, rotation, `mso-position-*`, `mso-wrap-distance-*`), `w10:wrap`, `v:imagedata`, `v:textbox`, `v:textpath` (WordArt watermark text: one centred paragraph). **Text-box paragraphs are no longer flattened into extra body paragraphs after the host paragraph** (they live in `DShape.blocks`). Theme colours use the Office default theme map (the document's own `theme1.xml` colours are not read).
- **Renderer (`DocxHtml.para` / `place` / `shapeHtml`).** Floating objects are emitted BEFORE their paragraph (a `<div>` or `<table>` inside a `<p>` would close the `<p>`): `square / tight` -> CSS float (side: `wrapText` left / right, else alignment, else offset past the middle of the content / page width); `tb` -> own block (centred / right / left margin); in front of or behind the text (`none`, or `behindDoc`) -> **print layout:** an empty marker `<span class=ao data-o=N>` in the text plus an entry in `<div id=objs>`; **simple / reading view:** a zero-height `position:relative` box above the paragraph with the object absolutely positioned inside (top = offset only for paragraph / line relative, else 0; left by alignment or offset; `z-index` -1 when behind; `.page` got `isolation:isolate` so -1 stays inside the page). Inline shapes without text stay inline (`<span>` inline-block); inline text boxes become a block before the paragraph. Shapes: rect, roundRect (radius = 1/6 of the short side), ellipse, line (inline SVG, flips honoured); any other preset is drawn as rect. Text-box paragraphs go through the normal `blocks()`.
- **Print script (`DocxPrintJs.objs`).** When a sheet is filled, every `.ao` marker in its clone (and in its header / footer boxes) is measured; markers whose x lies outside the text area belong to another page (the clone holds the whole flow) and are skipped. The object is cloned from `#objs` and placed on the SHEET in page coordinates: horizontal reference page / margin / column (column index from the marker for multi-column sections) / character; vertical page / margin / paragraph (= marker top); `align` replaces the offset. Behind objects are inserted before the text layer, front objects after the header / footer boxes; both sorted by `z` (behind order is reversed, ignore). Removed again when the sheet is emptied. Header / footer watermarks work through the same path.
- **Fixture / tests.** `tools/office_selftest/sample_float.docx` (made by `make_sample_float.py`, needs Pillow): picture right with square wrap, text box left with square wrap, top-and-bottom rounded rectangle, behind-text watermark picture centred on the page. `--float` self-test in `Main.kt` (reader values + print / simple HTML checks). Browser check: `float_layout_mirror_chromium.py` (mirror HTML, see status row) or, after a build, `--print sample_float.docx out.html` + `print_layout_chromium.py`.
- **Honest limits.** Float placement in print layout is a CSS float (side only): a picture anchored to the PAGE with square wrap lands at the paragraph, not at the exact page coordinate; wrap polygons (tight) are not followed (rectangular); `wrapText="largest"` is treated as both sides; picture cropping (`a:srcRect`), picture effects, shape gradients / shadows / transparency (`alpha`, `lumMod`), custom geometry and presets other than the four above, rotated float bounding boxes, text-box auto-fit and vertical text are not done; text boxes are clipped to their size (as Word); a float taller than the rest of its page is not pushed to the next page by the browser in every case; objects behind / in front of text whose anchor paragraph sits in a table cell use the marker position (fine) but their relV `paragraph` is the marker line. Linked text boxes (`w:txbxContent` split over several boxes) are not joined. In reading mode floats are capped at 45 percent of the width.
- **Device test (open, needs a CI build first).** Open `sample_float.docx` in Print layout: picture on the right with text wrapping on its left, text box on the left, red rounded bar centred, pale-blue watermark centred on the page behind the text; scroll + pinch; rotate. Same file in Simple view: floats left / right, watermark box behind the paragraph (approximate). A real Word file with a header watermark (WordArt DRAFT) shows it on every page. Search finds text-box text.

#### Part 7 notes (2026-10-24) - Tables, tabs, theme
- **`core/office/Theme.kt` (new, JVM).** `Theme.parse(MiniXml?)` reads `clrScheme` (12 slots, `srgbClr` / `sysClr lastClr`) + major / minor Latin font; `Theme.DEFAULT` = Office 2013+ theme. `color(name)` accepts Word names (`dark1 light1 text1 background1 text2 hyperlink ...`) and DrawingML names (`tx1 bg1 dk2 lt2 accent1..6 hlink folHlink`); `byIndex(i)` = Excel theme index (0 lt1, 1 dk1, 2 lt2, 3 dk2, 4-9 accents) for Part 9. Maths on HSL lightness: `word(themeColor, tintHex, shadeHex)` (tint: L*t + (1-t), shade: L*s; checked: accent1 4472C4 + tint 99 = 8FAADC, + shade BF = 2F5496), `draw(scheme, rgb, lumMod, lumOff, tint, shade)` for DrawingML (1/1000 percent), `excelTint(rgb, tint)` for Excel `tint` -1..1.
- **Model (`DocxModel.kt`).** `DBorder(style, sz eighths-pt, color)`, `DBorders` (top / left / bottom / right / insideH / insideV, `overlay`), `DTab(pos twips, kind, leader)`; `DCell` gained final `fill`, `bT bL bB bR`, `mar`, `vAlign`; `DRow.height`; `DTable.resolved / anyBorder / jc / indent / mar`; `DPara.tabs / fill / bdr`. `DTable.borders` stays the legacy guess (style name contains "grid" or direct `tblBorders`), used by .doc / .odt / .pptx and by reading mode.
- **Reader.** Theme: the document's own `theme1.xml` (found through the relationship, fallback `word/theme/theme1.xml`; Office default theme when missing) now drives run colours (`w:color` + `themeColor / themeTint / themeShade`; `auto` = none), `w:shd` (`themeFill*`; `val=solid` uses the colour), borders (`w:color` + theme attributes), theme fonts (unchanged behaviour: null when the package has no theme part) and the Part 6 DrawingML colours (`schemeClr` / `srgbClr` with `lumMod lumOff tint shade`, replaces the hard-coded Office palette). Table styles: `w:style w:type="table"` parsed into `TStyle` (basedOn chain, `tblStyleRowBandSize / ColBandSize`, `tblBorders`, `tblCellMar`, whole-table `pPr / rPr / tcPr`, every `tblStylePr` type); default table style (`w:default=1`) used when a table names none. `tblLook` (hex `val` bit mask and the attribute form; default 04A0). Conditional order, lowest first: whole table, band1/2Vert, band1/2Horz, firstCol, lastCol, firstRow, lastRow, nw / ne / sw / se corner. Text formatting from the style (`rPr`, `pPr` such as spacing after 0) is applied while the cell is parsed, BETWEEN docDefaults and the paragraph style (Word's order); `lastRow` text formatting (bold / italic / colour only) is applied after the table is complete because the row count is unknown while streaming. Final cell fill = direct `shd` > style conditional fills. Final cell borders per side = style `tblBorders` < conditional `tcBorders` < direct `tblBorders` < direct `tcBorders`; edge cells use top / left / bottom / right, inner edges `insideH / insideV`; `none / nil` clears. Also read: `tcMar`, `tblCellMar`, `vAlign`, `trHeight`, `tblInd`, table `jc`, paragraph `w:tabs` (style + direct, `clear` removes), paragraph `pBdr` (4 sides) and `shd`.
- **Renderer (`DocxHtml`).** Print and Simple view: a table with `resolved = true` draws its borders per cell (`border-top:Npx style #RRGGBB`, N = sz/6 px, double min 3 px), fill, padding from `tblCellMar / tcMar` (only when set), `vertical-align`, row height, table `jc` / `tblInd`; no `class=b` for those. Unresolved tables keep the old grid class. Reading mode: unchanged look (grid class when `borders || anyBorder`, no inline colours). Paragraph shading + borders (not in reading mode). Tabs (not in reading mode, only paragraphs without a list label): tab runs are assigned to stops in order (custom stops first, then 0.5 inch default stops; decimal counts as right, bar ignored) using an estimate of the text widths (chars x font size x 0.5). Layout modes: (1) last stop right-aligned, earlier ones left -> `display:flex` row, earlier segments `min-width` boxes, then a flexible leader span (`border-bottom:1px dotted / dashed / solid` for dot, middleDot / hyphen / underscore, 2px for heavy) and the right segment; (2) centre + right -> `display:grid;grid-template-columns:1fr auto 1fr` (header line); (3) lone centre stop -> same grid; (0) everything else (left stops, defaults) -> inline-block `min-width` boxes, `inline-flex` with a leader when the stop has one. The element gets `margin-left = max(0, left indent + first line)` and no text-indent; positions are relative to it.
- **Fixture / tests.** `tools/office_selftest/sample_styles.docx` (`make_sample_styles.py`, stdlib only): custom theme (accent1 1F6F8B, accent2 E4572E, Cambria / Calibri), Heading 1 with theme colour + shade and an accent2 bottom rule, banded table style (first row white-on-accent1, band1Horz tint, first column accent2 bold, last row double rule + bold), direct cell shading + left border + vAlign, three TOC lines with right dot-leader tab, a left / centre / right line, two default-stop lines, a shaded paragraph with top and bottom borders. `--styles` self-test (44 checks: theme maths, reader values, HTML strings in print, simple and reading output). Browser check: `java -jar ost.jar --print tools/office_selftest/sample_styles.docx out.html` + `python3 tools/office_selftest/print_layout_chromium.py out.html shot` (absolute paths); compare with `soffice --headless --convert-to pdf`.
- **Honest limits.** Tab positions use a text-width ESTIMATE, so a long segment may land on a different stop than in Word; centre / right stops in combinations other than [right last] or [centre, right] fall back to left boxes; tabs inside tables / text boxes work only as far as the cell is wide; no leader for tabs in reading mode; `lastRow` / `lastCol` conditional formatting of text is limited to bold / italic / colour (lastRow) and works fully for `lastCol`; `w:tblStylePr` inside a table style carrying `tblPr` borders is ignored (only `tcPr` borders); table style `tblW`, `tblLayout`, `cnfStyle` and row-level `trPr` banding are ignored; `w:tblInd` / `jc` are applied as margins (the table keeps its grid width); paragraph border `w:space` distances are replaced by a fixed 1px / 4px padding; `w:shd` patterns other than clear / solid use the fill colour; no cell diagonal borders; theme colours in run shading / highlights and in header / footer parts use the same theme (one per document). Pre-existing: theme `fontScheme` east-asian / complex-script fonts are ignored.
- **Device test (open, needs a CI build first).** Open `sample_styles.docx` in Print layout and Simple view: header row dark teal with white bold text, light-teal banded rows, orange bold first column, yellow cell with a red left bar, double line above "Total", teal rules above / below the table; three TOC lines with dotted leaders reaching the right margin; "Left / Centre / Right" spread across the width; shaded paragraph with orange rules. Reading mode: plain grid table, tabs as gaps. Then a real Word file with a built-in table style ("Grid Table 4 - Accent 1", "List Table 3") and a Word-generated TOC.

#### Part 8 notes (2026-10-24) - Comments, tracked changes, internal links
- **Model (`DocxModel.kt`).** `DRun.rev` (0 none, 1 inserted, 2 deleted), `DRun.revBy` (author), `DRun.cmt` (comma separated ids of the comment ranges covering the run), new run kind `DRun.CMT` (marker at the `w:commentReference`, text = comment id); `sameStyle` compares all three (so differently marked runs never merge). `DPara.bookmarks` (names of `w:bookmarkStart` in the paragraph). New `DComment(id, author, initials, date, text)`; `DDoc.comments` (LinkedHashMap, file order = the number shown, position + 1) and `DDoc.hasRev`. `DPara.text` (so `plainText`, search, export) now EXCLUDES deleted text = the final text.
- **Reader (`DocxReader.kt`).** `w:ins` / `w:moveTo` and `w:del` / `w:moveFrom` (new `revWrap`) mark every run inside with kind + author; `w:delText` is read like `w:t`. Before, `del` was dropped and `ins` was plain. `commentRangeStart / End` keep a set of open ids (`openCmts`, ranges may span paragraphs; cleared after the body so an unclosed range cannot leak into headers / notes), `commentReference` adds the CMT marker run. `readComments` reads `word/comments.xml` (paragraphs of one comment joined by a newline; the `annotationRef` run has no text). Bookmarks: `bookmarkStart` -> `DPara.bookmarks` (also in headings of a TOC). Complex fields `HYPERLINK "url"` / `HYPERLINK \l "bookmark"` / `HYPERLINK "url" \l "frag"` now turn the result runs into a link (`hyperField`), so older TOCs and cross references are clickable too. `w:hyperlink w:anchor` already worked (link `#name`).
- **Renderer (`DocxHtml.kt`).** New parameter `markup` on `render` and `renderPrint` (default `MARK_SHOW`; `MARK_FINAL`, `MARK_ORIGINAL`), kept in a ThreadLocal like `PRINT`. **Show markup:** insertion = underline, deletion = line-through, both in one colour per author (6 colours, lighter set in the dark reading theme); commented text has a pale yellow background (only when the run has no highlight of its own); after the commented range a small `[n]` badge `<a class=cm href="lsoffice://comment/ID">`. **Final:** deleted runs are not drawn, a paragraph that consists only of deleted runs is dropped (as Word does), no markers. **Original:** the opposite (inserted runs / paragraphs gone, deleted text plain). Bookmarks are emitted as an empty `<a id="name"></a>` right after the paragraph's opening tag (not repeated when a paragraph is re-opened after a page break in print layout).
- **Viewer (`OfficeViewerActivity.kt`).** Field `markup` (per open viewer, not saved). "..." menu, only for Word-type documents: **Tracked changes: Show markup / Final / Original** (only when `hasRev`; a single-choice dialog, then re-render, same scroll position) and **Comments (N)** (only when there are comments; one dialog listing number, author, date, text). A tap on a `[n]` badge opens that one comment (`lsoffice://comment/<id>`; the URL handler now looks at the HOST, so `lsoffice://sheet/<n>` still switches sheets). In-page `#bookmark` links: Simple / Reading mode use the browser's own fragment scrolling (same mechanism as footnote links); print layout already resolves `#id` links to the right page in `DocxPrintJs.links()` (Part 5), and the bookmark anchors sit inside the cloned flow so that lookup finds them.
- **Fixture and self-test.** `tools/office_selftest/make_sample_review.py` -> `sample_review.docx`: heading with bookmark, a `w:hyperlink w:anchor` TOC line, a sentence with 2 comments + 1 insertion (Alice) + 1 deletion (Bob), one fully inserted and one fully deleted paragraph, a second bookmarked heading, a complex `HYPERLINK \l` field. `Main.kt --review` (`reviewTest`) checks the model (comments, authors, runs, bookmarks, links), the Show / Final / Original HTML, the print render and `plainText`.
- **Honest limits / not done.** (1) Nothing here was compiled or run (see section 8). (2) Comments are listed in a dialog, not as side bubbles in the margin (a phone has no margin; Print layout could get bubbles later). (3) Only run-level insert / delete are shown: paragraph-mark changes (`w:rPr/w:ins`), table row / cell insert / delete (`w:trPr/w:ins`), formatting changes (`w:rPrChange`, `w:pPrChange`) and moves as a pair are NOT marked (moved text shows as deleted + inserted, which is what Word shows too). (4) In Final / Original the tab layout of a line still counts hidden tabs (rare). (5) Comment replies (`commentsExtended`) are listed as separate comments, not threaded. (6) The web share page (`OfficeWeb.kt`) calls `render` with the default (Show markup): the `[n]` badges there do nothing. (7) The editor (`DocxEditor`) works on the XML and does not know about tracked changes: replacing text in a paragraph that contains `w:ins` / `w:del` rewrites it as plain text (pre-existing limit, now more visible). (8) TOC page numbers are the stored ones (never recalculated), `TOC` / `PAGEREF` fields keep their cached result.
- **Device test (open, needs a CI build first).** Open `sample_review.docx` (Simple view and Print layout): heading "Review sample", a TOC line "Second part ... 2" that jumps to the second heading when tapped; the sentence shows "quickly" underlined in one colour and "lazily" struck through in another colour, "jumps" and "yard" on a yellow background with badges [1] and [2]; the added paragraph underlined, the removed one struck through. Tap [1] -> dialog "Carol", text `Is "jumps" the right verb here?`; menu Comments (2) lists both. Menu Tracked changes -> Final: "jumps quickly across the yard.", no removed paragraph, no badges; -> Original: "jumps lazily across the yard.", no added paragraph. "Back to the first part" (field link) jumps to the first heading. A document without changes / comments shows neither menu item.

#### Part 9 notes (2026-10-24) - Excel A: theme colours, conditional formatting, notes, gridlines, widths
- **Model (`XlsxModel.kt`, all additions have defaults so `XlsReader` / `OdfReader` are untouched).** `XXf.borderId`; `XBSide(style, color)`, `XBorder(left, right, top, bottom, any)`; `XDxf(color, fill, bold, italic, underline, strike)`; `XCfvo`, `XCfRule` (ranges, type, priority, dxfId, operator, rank / percent / bottom, text, above / equalAvg, stopIfTrue, formulas, cfvo list, colours, dataBar min / max length); `XNote(ref, row, col, author, text)`; `XStyles.borders / dxfs`; `XSheet.showGrid / cf / notes / fileWidths / defaultColWidth`.
- **Reader (`XlsxReader.kt`).** Workbook theme (`theme` relationship, fallback `xl/theme/theme1.xml`, Office default when missing) -> `colorOf(x)`: `rgb` (ARGB, alpha ignored), `theme` index via `Theme.byIndex` + `tint` via `Theme.excelTint`, `indexed` (default 64-colour palette; 64 / 65 and `auto` = null = renderer default). Used for font colours, fills (`fgColor`), borders and dxfs (in a dxf the fill colour is `bgColor`). `borders` (left / right / top / bottom, `start` / `end` as left / right, style + colour), `dxfs` (font colour / b / i / u / strike, fill). Sheet: `sheetView showGridLines`, `sheetFormatPr defaultColWidth`, `conditionalFormatting` with `sqref` (the `x14` extension copies have no `sqref` attribute and are skipped), legacy comments via the sheet's `comments` relationship (threaded-comment warning text stripped). **Empty cells with a fill or border are now kept** (`boxed(st)`): before, `<c s="3"/>` without a value was dropped, so coloured / ruled blank cells were missing.
- **Column widths.** The xlsx `width` attribute already includes the 5 px padding: pixels = `width x digit width` (A = 14.7109375 -> 103 px). The old code used `width x 7 + 5`, and the brief says `+ 5`; that formula is for the width Excel shows in its dialog, NOT the file value, so xlsx now uses `round(width x mdw)` (`XSheet.fileWidths = true`); xls / ods keep the old formula. `mdw` = digit width of the default font (`XlsxHtml.digitWidth`: Calibri / Carlito 11 pt = 7 px, scaled by size; Arial 10 pt = 7 px; fallback 7). Missing columns use `defaultColWidth` or 64 px.
- **`XlsxCf.kt` (new, JVM).** `XlsxCf.evaluate(sheet, styles)` -> map key `row * 100000 + col` -> `CfOut` (fill, font colour, bold, italic, underline, strike, data bar 0..1 + colour). Rules run by priority, the lowest number wins per property, `stopIfTrue` is per cell. Types: cellIs (all 8 operators; operands = number / string / cell ref / formula), expression (comparisons, `+ - * / &`, `AND OR NOT IF MOD ROW COLUMN ISBLANK ISNUMBER ISTEXT ISEVEN ISODD ABS INT LEN`, relative refs shift from the first cell of the range, `$` pins), top10 (rank / percent / bottom, ties included), aboveAverage / below (`equalAverage`), duplicateValues / uniqueValues, containsText / notContainsText / beginsWith / endsWith, containsBlanks / containsErrors and their negations, colorScale (2 / 3 colours; min / max / num / percent / percentile / formula stops, linear RGB), dataBar (same stops; length = minLength + p x (maxLength - minLength), default 10 / 90 %, one solid colour, positive style only). Anything else (iconSet, timePeriod, stdDev, ranges / other sheets / unknown functions in a formula) is ignored without error.
- **Renderer (`XlsxHtml.kt`).** `render(sh, tabs, active, styles, fontBase, grid = null)`. Per cell: font effects, fill (CF fill > cell fill), data bar as `background-image:linear-gradient(90deg, colour N%, transparent N%)`, borders per side (thin 1 px, medium 2, thick 3, double 3 px double, dashed / dotted / hair mapped, default colour black). Gridlines are now `box-shadow:inset -1px -1px` on `td` (so explicit borders always win over the grid, which `border` conflicts in `border-collapse` did not guarantee); `grid = false` (or `showGridLines=0`) -> `<table class=ng>` removes them. Empty cells are still merged into colspans unless they have a fill / border / CF effect (then they are drawn). Notes: a red corner (`a.nt`, 14 px hit area) -> `lsoffice://note/<ref>`, plus a "Notes (n)" list with `id=n_<ref>` at the bottom of the sheet.
- **Viewer (`OfficeViewerActivity.kt`).** Excel menu: **Gridlines: turn off / on** (override kept while the viewer is open, not saved) and **Notes (n)** (only when the sheet has notes); tap on a red corner opens that note in a dialog. `OfficeWeb.kt` (browser share page) turns the corner links into `#n_<ref>` anchors of the notes list.
- **Fixture / test.** `tools/office_selftest/sample_cf.xlsx` (`make_sample_cf.py`, stdlib only): custom theme (accent1 1F6F8B, accent2 E4572E), header = theme fill + white theme font + medium bottom rule, 3-colour scale, data bar, cellIs > 50, top-2, expression `$B2>=$C2`, accent2 tint 0.6 text cell and blank cell, 2 comments, frozen header, custom widths, second sheet with `showGridLines=0` and a red thin box. `java -jar ost.jar --cf` (53 checks, includes a synthetic-sheet test of the evaluator: MOD(ROW()), between, AND / OR, above / below average, top / bottom percent, duplicates, text rules, blanks, ISBLANK, ignored rules).
- **Honest limits / not done.** (1) Android files not compiled / built / device-tested. (2) Excel's own number-format effects in rules (dxf `numFmt`, `border`) are ignored. (3) Only existing cells get CF (a rule never fills a cell that has no value and no style). (4) iconSet, negative-value data bars (axis, negative colour), gradient vs solid bar setting (`x14`), `stdDev`, `timePeriod` and formulas with ranges (`COUNTIF($A:$A,A1)>1`) are not done. (5) Theme tint uses the HSL maths of `Theme.excelTint`, which can differ by 1-2 levels per channel from Excel. (6) Row heights are still a fixed 22 px (row `ht`, wrapped rows not honoured). (7) Gridlines do not draw under coalesced empty cells (unchanged old look). (8) Notes show as text only: no rich formatting, no sizes, threaded replies not threaded. (9) Column width at a non-Calibri default font uses an estimated digit width.
- **Device test (open, needs a CI build first).** Open `sample_cf.xlsx`: header dark teal with white bold text and a heavy black line below; column Score red -> yellow -> green; column Sales blue bars (shortest at 50, longest at 300); Target cells 55 / 65 / 80 pink with dark red text; Rank 12 and 12 bold green on light green; "Echo" italic purple; "Tinted text" and F1 / F2 light orange; red corners on Score 12 and Target 65 -> tap = dialog "Tester: Check this value" / "Above target"; menu Notes (2) lists both. Tab NoGrid: no gridlines, one red box; menu "Gridlines: turn on" brings them back; the setting survives switching sheets. Then a real Excel file with a theme-coloured header and an Excel data bar.

#### Part 10 notes (2026-10-24) - Excel charts 1: column / bar / line
- **New `core/office/ChartModel.kt` (JVM).** Model: `XChartFrame(anchor, chart)`, `XAnchor` (kind 0 twoCell: from / to cell + EMU offsets; 1 oneCell: from + `ext` cx x cy; 2 absolute: `pos` + `ext`), `XChart(title, plots, cat, value axes, legend r/l/t/b/null, kinds)`, `XChartPlot(type bar|line|other:<kind>, horizontal, grouping clustered|stacked|percentStacked|standard, gapWidth, overlap, markers, labels, secondary, series)`, `XChartSeries(name, cats, vals (null = gap), color "#RRGGBB", marker, smooth, labels, fmt)`, `XChartAxis(deleted, title, min, max, major, fmt, grid, reverse, tickLabels)`. `ChartParser.parseDrawing(pkg, drawingPart, theme, resolver)` reads `twoCellAnchor / oneCellAnchor / absoluteAnchor` (anchors without a chart, e.g. pictures, are skipped: Part 11), follows `c:chart r:id` through the drawing's rels; `parseChart` reads plots (`barChart`, `bar3DChart`, `lineChart`, `line3DChart` as bar / line; every other `*Chart` is kept as `other:<kind>`), series (`tx`, `cat`, `val`, `spPr`, `marker/symbol`, `smooth`, `dLbls/showVal`), axes by `axId` (`scaling min/max/orientation`, `majorUnit`, `numFmt`, `majorGridlines`, `delete`, `tickLblPos`, title) and title / legend. A small element tree (`ChartParser.N`, capped at 60,000 nodes, chart part capped at 4 MB, 30 charts per sheet) keeps the parser simple.
- **Values.** The cached `pt` lists are used. **Fallback:** when a series has no cache (openpyxl and several generators write none) `XlsxReader.refCells` reads the referenced plain range (`Sheet1!$B$2:$B$9`, `'My sheet'!A1:A5`) from the workbook cells (max 5,000 cells, sheets cached per call, charts of the other sheet are not read, so no recursion). Text cells plot as 0 like Excel, empty cells are gaps. Category labels from a number cache use the cache `formatCode` (dates work).
- **Colours.** Explicit `srgbClr` / `schemeClr` (+ `lumMod lumOff tint shade` through `Theme.draw`) / `sysClr`; bars use the fill, lines the line colour. Automatic colours: accent1..6 of the workbook theme, then 60 % and 80 % + 20 % lightness rounds (like Excel).
- **`core/office/ChartSvg.kt` (JVM).** `ChartSvg.render(chart, w, h)` -> one `<svg>` string, no JS, font `inherit` (the page font). Auto axis like Excel: starts at 0 unless all data sit in the top sixth, step 1 / 2 / 5 x 10^k with about one tick per 38 px, 5 % head-room above the maximum; `min / max / majorUnit` from the file win; 100 % stacked is 0..100 %. Clustered / stacked / 100 % stacked column and bar (gapWidth, overlap, negative values from the zero line, horizontal bars with the first category at the bottom, `orientation maxMin` reverses), line (stacked cumulative, straight or smooth Catmull-Rom curves, gaps at empty cells, markers circle / square / diamond / triangle / x; automatic marker only when the plot has `<marker val=1>`), bar + line combos on one axis pair, optional value labels (`showVal`, series or plot level), major gridlines, tick labels (`low / none`, number format from the axis, else the series), category labels rotate -45 degrees when they do not fit and skip every n-th label when crowded, axis titles, legend r / l / t / b (wraps), title (shrinks to fit). Numbers are written with `Math.round` + `Double.toString` only, so a Turkish default locale cannot put commas into the SVG (self-test checks tr-TR).
- **Not drawn (stated in the chart itself).** Plots that are not bar / line (pie, doughnut, area, scatter, radar, bubble, stock ... = Part 11) and plots on a **secondary axis** (a note "Not drawn: ..." at the bottom-left; a chart with nothing drawable becomes a box with the title and "<kind> chart: not drawn yet"). Also not done: per-point colours (`dPt`, `varyColors`), trendlines, error bars, data-label positions / number formats per point, axis crossing other than zero, logarithmic axes, 3-D look (3-D bar / line are drawn flat), chart fonts / sizes from `txPr`, plot-area fill, rounded corners, `c:style`, manual layout (`c:layout`), chartsheets (a whole sheet that is a chart), charts inside `mc:AlternateContent`.
- **Placement (`XlsxHtml.kt`).** `XSheet.charts` (new, default empty, so `XlsReader` / `OdfReader` are untouched). The charts are computed before the table, written after it as `<div class=ch style="left:..;top:..">` inside `.w` (`position:relative`, charts at `z-index:1`, below the sticky headers at 2 / 3): x = 28 px row-number column + the widths of the visible columns before the anchor column + offset / 9525; y = 22 px header row + 22 px x the visible rows before the anchor row + offset / 9525 (the same fixed 22 px rows the table uses, so a chart covers the same cells as in Excel, but its height in pixels differs from Excel's 20 px default rows); size from the anchor (twoCell) or `ext` (oneCell / absolute), clamped to 60..4000 px. `.w` gets `min-height` so charts below the last row are not cut off. A failing chart is skipped, never the sheet. `OfficeWeb.kt` (browser share page) shows the charts too (same `render`).
- **Fixture / test.** `tools/office_selftest/sample_chart.xlsx` (`make_sample_chart.py`, stdlib only): sheet Data = clustered column (2 series, title, axis titles, legend bottom, theme accent1 + rgb, gap 80, twoCellAnchor E2:M17) + line chart (circle markers on North, smooth South without markers, legend right, oneCellAnchor 480 x 300 at E19); sheet Bars = horizontal stacked bar WITHOUT cached values (cells fallback), axis 0..400 step 100, legend top; sheet Pie = pie (must show the "not drawn yet" box). `java -jar ost.jar --chart` (44 checks: parser, scales, SVG counts / colours / labels, tr-TR, HTML placement, broken XML). `python3 tools/office_selftest/chart_chromium.py <outdir>` screenshots the HTML the self-test writes to `$TMP/ost_chart_0..2.html`.
- **Honest limits.** (1) Android files not built by CI / not device-tested (only JVM code changed this part). (2) Pixel positions assume the viewer's 22 px rows; wrapped / taller rows are not honoured (known since Part 9). (3) Text widths in the SVG are estimated (0.55 em per glyph), so label rotation / truncation can differ slightly from Excel. (4) LibreOffice smooths all lines of a chart when the first series asks for it; we follow each series' own `smooth` flag (Excel does too). (5) Very large charts (many thousands of points) are not decimated.
- **Device test (open, needs a CI build first).** Open `sample_chart.xlsx`: tab Data shows a column chart (teal + orange bars, title "Sales by month", axis titles Month / Units, legend below) from cell E2 and a line chart (North with dots, South smooth, legend right) from E19; both scroll with the grid and stay under the frozen headers. Tab Bars: horizontal stacked bars Jan (bottom) to Jun (top), axis 0..400, legend on top. Tab Pie: a box titled "Share" saying "Pie chart: not drawn yet". Then a real Excel file with a column chart and a line chart (and one saved by openpyxl: no cached values).

#### Part 11 notes (2026-10-24) - Excel charts 2 + images: pie / doughnut, area, scatter, pictures, shapes
- **Model (`ChartModel.kt`, every addition has a default, so older callers compile unchanged).** `XChartSeries` + `xs` (scatter x values from `xVal`, cache first, then the cells), `ptColors` (pie / doughnut: `dPt` colour or the automatic accent cycle, per point), `pct` / `catName` (data labels `showPercent` / `showCatName`, series or plot level), `line` (false when the series line is `noFill`). `XChartPlot` + `hole` (doughnut `holeSize`, default 50) and `firstAngle` (`firstSliceAng`). Plot types are now `bar | line | area | pie | doughnut | scatter | other:<kind>` (`pieChart`, `pie3DChart`, `doughnutChart`, `areaChart`, `area3DChart`, `scatterChart`). A series takes `cat` or `xVal` and `val` or `yVal`. Scatter: the first `axId` is the x axis (`XChart.cat`), the second the y axis (`XChart.value`). Pie / doughnut have no axes and no longer set the "first axis pair" that decides which plots are secondary. A series without line / fill colour now takes the marker fill (`marker/spPr`), then the automatic colour. `ChartParser.tree`, `anchorOf`, `colorOf` are `internal` now and the element tree also keeps `embed`, `prst`, `sz`.
- **`ChartSvgMore.kt` (new, `object ChartSvg2`, JVM).** `ChartSvg.render` calls `ChartSvg2.render` first; it returns null (= old code path) when the chart has any non-secondary bar / line plot, so combos with area / pie / scatter are still drawn by `ChartSvg` with the "Not drawn: ..." note. One chart shows ONE family: pie / doughnut, else area, else scatter; other kinds present are listed in the note. Pie: slices clockwise from `firstSliceAng`, white 1.5 px separators, first series only; negative values use their absolute value; labels inside at 62 % of the radius (slice >= 5 %) or outside; label text = category / value / percent (`Math.round`, "39%") joined by ", "; text colour chosen by fill brightness; legend lists the categories. Doughnut: one ring per series (first series innermost), hole from `holeSize`. Area: standard (later series in front), stacked, 100 % stacked; categories "on tick marks" (first / last on the plot edges, like Excel's default); axis, grid, tick labels, titles, legend as in `ChartSvg`; blanks count as 0. Scatter: numeric x and y axes with the same automatic scale as `ChartSvg`, grid per axis flag, lines (straight or smooth) unless the series line is `noFill`, markers (default circle, `none` hides them), value labels, axis lines at zero. Text: `font-family:inherit`; numbers written with `Math.round` + `Double.toString` only (locale safe). Helpers of `ChartSvg` (`f`, `esc`, `tw`, `cut`, `marker`, constants) went from `private` to `internal`.
- **`DrawingModel.kt` (new, JVM).** `DrawingParser.parse(pkg, drawingPart, theme)` -> `XDrawingExtras(pics, shapes)`. Pictures: `xdr:pic` -> `blip r:embed` -> media bytes (png / jpeg / gif / bmp / webp / svg, max 3 MB each, 30 per sheet; emf / wmf / tiff skipped). Shapes: `xdr:sp` (max 60): preset geometry name, fill (`solidFill`, first gradient stop, else the style `fillRef` unless `noFill`), outline (`ln` colour, else style `lnRef` unless idx 0 / `noFill`), text (paragraphs joined by newlines, 2,000 chars), text colour (first run colour, else style `fontRef`), size (first `sz`). Shapes with no text, no fill and no outline are dropped. `DrawingParser.base64` is an own encoder (java.util.Base64 needs API 26, minSdk is 24). `XSheet` got `pics` and `shapes` (defaults empty); `XlsxReader.readExtras` fills them (only when `withCharts`, so the chart cell-resolver sheets stay light).
- **Placement (`XlsxHtml.kt`).** Same coordinates as the charts (28 px row-number column, 22 px header row and rows, hidden rows / columns skipped). Picture = `<div class=ch><img src="data:...">` sized from the anchor; budget 8 MB of picture bytes per sheet, the rest are skipped. Shape = absolutely placed flex box: `ellipse` -> 50 % radius, `roundRect` -> 12 px, every other preset (arrows, triangles, callouts, lines) as a plain rectangle; text centred when the shape has a fill, left-aligned otherwise (text boxes); `white-space:pre-wrap`; text colour by fill brightness when the file gives none. The sheet scroll box grows to fit pictures / shapes below the last row. `OfficeWeb.kt` (browser share page) shows them too (same `render`).
- **Fixture / tests.** `tools/office_selftest/sample_chart2.xlsx` (`make_sample_chart2.py`, stdlib only, run from the repo root): Pie sheet (pie with percent labels and a gold `dPt` slice + doughnut, hole 55, first slice 90 degrees, legend bottom), Area (stacked, legend bottom), Scatter (A = diamonds only, B = smooth line + circles, titled axes), Pics (60 x 40 PNG, rounded box with white text, oval, two-line text box). `java -jar ost.jar --chart2` = about 40 checks (parser values, colours, SVG element counts, labels 39 / 26 / 19 / 13 / 3 %, HTML boxes, data URI, base64 vectors). The Part 10 check "pie: placeholder" in `--chart` now expects a drawn pie.
- **Honest limits.** (1) **Nothing of Part 11 has been compiled or run** (see the status row); expect a few compiler errors on the first build. (2) Area + bar / line combos: the area plot is NOT drawn in those (note at the bottom). Bar / line + pie combos likewise. (3) Not done: secondary axis, data labels on area, bubble / radar / stock / surface / 3-D look, exploded slices, `dPt` colours for bar / line points, leader lines, label positions, gradient fills (first stop only), picture crop / rotation / effects, grouped shapes (`xdr:grpSp`), connectors / lines (`xdr:cxnSp`), shape text bold / italic / per-run formatting, pictures inside shapes. (4) Pie percent uses whole numbers (Excel's default `0%`); a custom label number format is ignored. (5) LibreOffice's PDF of the fixture colours only the `dPt` slice of the pie (it leaves the other slices unfilled when the file gives no automatic colours); Excel colours all slices, so our accent-cycle colours are the expected look, not a mismatch.
- **Device test (open, needs a CI build first).** Open `sample_chart2.xlsx`: tab Pie shows a pie (teal / gold / grey / gold / light blue, labels 39% 26% 19% 13% and a small "3%" outside, legend right) from cell D2 and a doughnut below it (hole about half the radius, legend below); tab Area: two stacked bands (teal North below, orange South above), months on the edges of the plot, legend below; tab Scatter: orange diamonds (Series A, no line) and a teal smooth line with dots (Series B), axis titles Input / Reading; tab Pics: a gradient picture in B3, a teal rounded box with white text, an orange oval and a plain two-line text box. Then a real Excel file with a pie and a picture.

#### Part 12 notes (2026-10-24) - Navigation: outline, sheet list, formula bar, page thumbnails
- **Word outline.** `DocxModel.kt` `DPara.hid` (-1 = none). New `core/office/DocxOutline.kt` (JVM): `DocxOutline.build(doc)` walks top-level paragraphs and table cells (depth <= 2, max 500), numbers every non-empty heading (`heading > 0`, deleted tracked-change text left out), sets `hid`, returns `OutlineItem(id, level 1..6, text <= 90 chars)`. `DocxHtml.para` writes ` id=lsh<hid>` on the heading element (works in simple, reading and print layout; in print layout the id lives in the hidden stage, the page clones have their ids stripped as before). `OfficeViewerActivity.build()` calls it BEFORE rendering and keeps `outline`.
- **Jump.** More menu > "Outline (N)" (id 16) opens a list (indented by level); a tap calls `scrollToId`: JavaScript is switched on ONLY for that one `evaluateJavascript` call and back to its old state in the callback (the page has no scripts of its own outside print layout); simple / reading page: `getElementById(..).scrollIntoView()`; print layout: `window.__pl.goId(id)` (new in `DocxPrintJs`: finds the heading in the stage, `pageOf`, scrolls that page sheet into view).
- **Excel formula bar.** `XlsxHtml` wraps the text of non-empty cells in `<a class=cl href="lsoffice://cell/ROW/COL">` (0-based, max 20,000 links per sheet, CSS `a.cl{color:inherit;text-decoration:none}`). The activity routes host `cell` to `showCell`: a one-to-two-line native bar under the toolbar (visible for sheets only) shows `B3   fx  =SUM(...)   ->  value`, or `B3   raw value` when there is no formula. Empty cells are not tappable (no link); the selected cell is not highlighted (that needs a re-render).
- **Sheet list.** More menu > "Sheets (N)" (id 17, only when the workbook has more than one sheet): dialog at the bottom of the screen, current sheet ticked, hidden sheets listed only after the neutral button "Show hidden (n)"; very hidden sheets never. A tap opens that sheet through `showSheet` (the tab bar in the page is unchanged).
- **Page thumbnail strip (print layout only).** `DocxPrintJs.strip()`: a fixed bottom bar with one small white box per page (aspect ratio of the page's section, page number inside), tap = scroll to that page, the page under the middle of the screen gets an orange border and the bar scrolls to keep it centred (updated on `scroll`, one `requestAnimationFrame` at a time). Sizes are multiplied by `innerWidth / screen.width` so the boxes stay readable when the page is shrunk to the screen. Not shown for a one-page document. In Exact PDF mode nothing was added (the PDF viewer's overview grid is the thumbnail view). A failure of `strip()` is caught and left in `window.__plStripError`.
- **Tests.** `java -jar ost.jar --nav`: outline ids / text / level on `sample_styles.docx`, `id=lsh0` in simple and print HTML, stable numbering on a second `build`, the print script has `goId` and the strip and no dollar sign, `lsoffice://cell/` links in `sample_chart2.xlsx`. NOT run (no compiler).
- **Honest limits.** (1) Nothing of Part 12 was compiled or run. (2) Outline: headings inside text boxes, headers, footnotes and tables deeper than two levels are not listed; no collapse / expand; in print layout a heading whose paragraph is split over two pages jumps to the page where it starts. (3) The thumbnail boxes show page numbers, not a miniature of the content. (4) The formula bar is read-only (editing stays in "Edit cell..."); merged cells link at their top-left cell only; cells beyond 20,000 per sheet are not tappable. (5) Opening a hidden sheet from the list works, but the in-page tab bar does not list it.
- **Device test (open, needs a CI build first).** Open `sample_styles.docx`: More > Outline > the heading is listed, tap scrolls there; turn Print layout on, open the outline again (jumps to the right page sheet) and check the strip: a box per page, the current one orange, tap jumps. Open `sample_chart2.xlsx`: More > Sheets lists 4 sheets, a tap switches; tap a cell with text: the bar under the toolbar shows its reference and value; open a real workbook with formulas and a hidden sheet (Show hidden works).
